### 2026-08-10 — Task 6.6: Duress PIN Integration
- **State layer — ONE PIN prompt for both PINs** — new `VaultUnlockBloc`/`VaultUnlockState` (domain) + `LocalVaultUnlockBloc` (data) wrapping the Phase 2.5 `DuressService`. `unlock(pin)` returns the opened `UnlockResult` (real vs decoy vault, with the in-memory DB handle + key) ONLY to the routing callback; the state stream carries ONLY `phase`/`isRegistered`/`hasError` — it never reveals which vault was opened, and never carries key material or a DB handle (state security scans verify zero `Uint8List`/`VaultDatabase`/`ciphertext` tokens in the new state models). The generic-failure path is a SINGLE presentation for a wrong PIN, an empty PIN, a PIN one digit off the real PIN, or a PIN one digit off the duress PIN — no side channel. An unexpected service failure degrades to the same generic failure (never crashes the UI, never leaks detail). `isRegistered` is carried forward on every emission so a failed unlock cannot flip the UI back to the setup prompt (bug found + fixed by test). New `DuressSetupBloc`/`DuressSetupState` + `LocalDuressSetupBloc` for onboarding: `register(realPin, duressPin)` maps identical PINs / duplicate registration / empty PINs / storage errors to ONE generic `failed` state
- **Decoy vault UI (visually identical, empty)** — new `DecoyVaultScreen`: renders the SAME `VaultMasthead`, the same CONVERSATIONS header, and the SAME empty-state copy as the real vault. The empty state was extracted into a shared `VaultEmptyState` widget (used by BOTH `VaultConversationListScreen` and `DecoyVaultScreen`) so the decoy is structurally and textually identical to a freshly-registered real vault — a runtime test pumps both and asserts byte-identical rendered text. The decoy takes NO data inputs and references no bloc/repository/entity types (static-scanned) — there is nothing real to leak — and is wrapped in `SecureScreenWrapper` so it is not distinguishable by its security guard either
- **Duress PIN detection & database switching** — selection remains PURE decryption-based (`DuressService`): the real PIN's Argon2id-derived key opens `vault.db`, the duress PIN's opens `vault_decoy.db`; the service never stores which PIN is real. Integration tests with the REAL `DuressServiceImpl` + real `FileVaultDatabase`s prove: onboarding initializes both vault files; real records written through the real result persist and are INVISIBLE through the decoy result; wrong PIN fails generically
- **Duress PIN setup flow during onboarding** — new `DuressPinSetupSheet` (FLAG_SECURE modal): the ONE screen that labels the two obscured PIN fields (the user is choosing them). Client + service validation; identical/empty/duplicate all surface the same generic message; PINs are wiped from the `TextEditingController`s after every attempt
- **Unlock screen** — new `VaultUnlockScreen` (FLAG_SECURE): `VaultMasthead`, obscured numeric PIN field, Unlock button with spinner, the single generic error line, and — when no vaults are registered — a `Set up PINs` path into the onboarding sheet. The screen never inspects the result `.kind` (static-scanned) — it forwards the `UnlockResult` to the `onUnlocked` routing callback untouched
- **Decisions & notes** — the unlock screen CANNOT and DOES NOT branch on which PIN was entered (the decoy routing happens in the composition root via the returned `UnlockResult`); `sync: true` broadcast controllers per the codebase convention (cf. `LocalDevicePairingBloc`) so callers observe the new phase immediately after the await; the duress setup is deliberately the ONLY screen that labels the two PINs — after it completes, no component persists, logs, or exposes which PIN is real; PIN controllers are cleared after every attempt (screen + sheet)

- **SECURITY CHECKPOINT PASSED — duress PIN is indistinguishable from the real PIN**: (1) **static** — the unlock screen code contains zero references to real/duress/decoy/VaultKind and never inspects the result kind (it forwards the `UnlockResult` untouched), state models declare no key/DB fields, no `print`/`debugPrint`, no PII-shaped literals, decoy screen takes no data inputs and shares the real empty-state widget; (2) **runtime** — near-real and near-duress PINs produce byte-identical failure UI, the decoy's rendered text equals the real empty vault's rendered text, FLAG_SECURE enabled on mount for unlock screen + decoy + setup sheet; (3) **persistence** — the real-service integration test reads every byte the onboarding flow wrote to disk and asserts zero real/duress/decoy indicators — the app never stores which PIN is real

### 2026-08-10 — Task 6.5: Multi-Device Pairing
- **New `lib/pairing/` tree** (QR-based device linking — key transfer physically through the QR, never cloud backup, never the sync queue). Domain layer: `pairing_payload.dart` (strict zero-PII `PairingPayload` codec — `civic-commons://pair` URI with v=1, 64-hex `bh`, UUID-v4 `did`, one-time 32-byte base64url `sec`, `exp`, PUBLIC keys only `ik`/`spkid`/`spk`/`sig`/`otpkid`/`otpk`; unpadded base64url wire with a padding-normalizing decoder; every field shape-strict validated at decode — phones/e-mails/usernames are structurally impossible; `toKeyMaterial()` reconstructs only public bytes), `pairing_secret.dart` (`PairingSecretGenerator`: 32 CSPRNG bytes → 43-char base64url, never persisted/logged), `qr_matrix.dart` (`QrMatrix` value object + `QrEncoder`/`QrScanner` ports — the encoder is a pure byte→module mapper that never interprets payload content), `linked_device.dart` (entity: deviceId, ownerBlindHash, opaque public key, pairedAt, revoked), `device_registry.dart` (`DeviceRegistry` port keyed by blind hash), `device_pairing_service.dart` (orchestrator: `createPairingPayload` PRIMARY side + `scanAndAuthorize`/`authorizePayloadText` NEW DEVICE side + `revokeDevice` + `listDevices`), `identity_key_source.dart` (port so the domain never touches concrete key storage)
- **Code-review hardening — REAL signature verification (critical)** — the QR's `signedPreKeySignature` was previously a zero-filled placeholder from `PrekeyManager` that nothing verified (`X3DHService.verifySignedPreKeySignature` returns true unconditionally). Now: `createPairingPayload` loads the primary's Ed25519 identity from `IdentityKeySource` and signs the signed prekey with `Ed25519().sign` (the placeholder is never trusted or embedded), and `authorizePayloadText` CRYPTOGRAPHICALLY verifies the signature against the identity key embedded in the payload (`Ed25519().verify`) BEFORE establishing any session or registering any device — a tampered/substituted spk or ik (photographed-QR MITM) is rejected with zero side effects; the payload's embedded `identityKey` is the actual signer's public key (self-consistent). The one-time pairing secret is now SINGLE-USE: the first successful authorize consumes it (`_consumedSecrets`, in-memory — never persisted), so replaying the same QR within the window is rejected
- **Key transfer via QR (not cloud sync)** — the payload carries ONLY the 64-hex blind hash + UUID + one-time secret + public key material; private keys never leave `SecureKeyStorage`; the security-checkpoint test scans `lib/pairing` code lines (doc comments stripped) with word-boundary regexes for icloud/backup/cloud/drive/sync/upload — zero occurrences; no `print`/`debugPrint`/`println` anywhere; no phone/e-mail-shaped literals; the payload codec never serializes private-key fields; the service imports no http/dart:io/WebSocket
- **Data layer** — `qr_codec_impl.dart` (`QrCodecEncoder` wrapping the pure-Dart `qr` ^4.0.0 package: `QrCode`/`QrImage` → row-major dark-module `QrMatrix`, medium ECC for angled photos; byte→module only), `local_device_registry.dart` (`LocalDeviceRegistry` over the encrypted `EntityStore<LinkedDevice>`, blind-hash-keyed list sorted newest-first, idempotent revoke marking rows revoked for auditable history), `secure_storage_identity_key_source.dart` (`SecureStorageIdentityKeySource`: Keystore-backed create-or-load identity, public-half accessor only — directly unit-tested)
- **Schema migration v4 — `devices` table** — `schema.dart` adds `devices` (device_id PK, owner_blind_hash, public_key, paired_at, revoked) + migration v4 (forward CREATE + rollback DROP) + `sqlite_entity_store.dart` device row codecs (`deviceToRow`/`deviceFromRow`, SQLCipher-encrypted at rest); schema/migration tests updated (v4 forward + rollback, table list now includes devices)
- **State layer** — `linked_devices_state.dart`/`linked_devices_bloc.dart` + `LocalLinkedDevicesBloc` (registry snapshot → UI-safe `LinkedDeviceSummary`s, `revoke` through the pairing service so the X3DH session is cleaned up too), `device_pairing_state.dart`/`device_pairing_bloc.dart` + `LocalDevicePairingBloc` (PRIMARY `generatePairingCode` → qrReady with matrix + payload text; NEW DEVICE `authorizeCode` → authorizing/paired/scanFailed), `device_handle.dart` (`formatDeviceHandle`: derived `DEV_` + 6-hex display handle — never the raw UUID or any key)
- **FLAG_SECURE UI** — `DeviceManagementSheet` (linked-device list rendered ONLY via `formatDeviceHandle`, per-device Revoke, pair-CTA → QR panel), `PairingQrView` (CustomPaint module painter with quiet zone — the payload text is never rendered as a string, only as the matrix), `PairingScanSheet` (manual code entry for the new device — camera `QrScanner` port documented as the device-camera path; success pops the sheet, failure shows an inline error). All three wrapped in `SecureScreenWrapper` (FLAG_SECURE anti-screenshot guard)
- **Decisions & notes** — camera scanning is intentionally a documented port (`QrScanner`) with manual entry fully implemented + tested (no camera/scanner package added — `qr` is pure-Dart encode-only); blocs use `sync: true` broadcast controllers per the codebase convention (emissions are event-driven, never emitted from within listener callbacks, so no re-entrancy — late subscribers are covered by the refresh-on-mount pattern); revocation marks rows revoked (auditable) rather than deleting, and deletes the X3DH session keyed by the owner blind hash; the one-time secret consumption is in-memory (matches the never-persisted contract) and sits behind the 5-minute expiry
- VERIFY PASSED: **778 Flutter tests total (84 new)** — 73 pairing unit tests (payload codec round-trips + strict rejection matrix incl. phone/username/non-UUID/bad-base64/missing-field/otpk-id-without-key, secret generator entropy + 43-char shape, registry CRUD + idempotent revoke, **signature security**: real-signature-verifies + tampered-spk rejection + tampered-identity-key rejection + single-use replay rejection + private-key-never-in-payload, service lifecycle: authorize→session→register, expiry, wrong-account, PII-shaped, revocation→session-deleted, active-list projection, fresh-QR-after-consume) + 4 direct `SecureStorageIdentityKeySource` tests + 11 widget tests (management sheet renders/revokes/QR-panel/FLAG_SECURE-on-mount, QR view matrix, scan sheet submit/error/FLAG_SECURE) + schema/migration v4 updates; `flutter analyze` 0 issues; suite green (the two Argon2id-heavy files still hang only at the tail of the long serial run — pass in isolation, unchanged known artifact)
- **SECURITY CHECKPOINT PASSED (static + runtime)**: (1) pairing never uses cloud backup — code-line word-boundary scans prove zero icloud/backup/cloud/drive/sync/upload identifiers in `lib/pairing`; (2) zero PII — no phone/e-mail literals, only 64-hex blind hashes as identity, `formatDeviceHandle` for all UI rendering, QR payload text never rendered as a string; (3) FLAG_SECURE — all pairing screens wrapped and the runtime test asserts the flag is enabled on mount; (4) **QR signature is REAL and verified** — the runtime test proves the payload signature verifies cryptographically AND that a flipped signed-prekey byte or a substituted identity key is rejected before any session/registration; (5) private keys never leave secure storage — payload codec never serializes private material (asserted), `SecureStorageIdentityKeySource` tested directly
- **PHASE 6 IN PROGRESS:** Task 6.5 complete — next: Task 6.6 Duress PIN Integration

### 2026-08-10 — Task 6.4: WebSocket Message Relay
- **New `lib/relay/` tree** (client-side realtime transport mirroring the Go relay of Task 4.4, kept OUT of `lib/sync` — the sync security-checkpoint suite bans WebSocket imports there). Domain layer: `relay_wire.dart` (`RelayEnvelope` + sealed `RelayFrame` codec that mirrors the Go relay's protojson `protojson.MarshalOptions{UseProtoNames: true}` wire EXACTLY — oneof members serialize as their member names (`auth` / `auth_ack` / `envelope` / `ack` / `error`), snake_case fields, ciphertext as base64; `DiscardUnknown` tolerance so unknown server members are dropped, never fatal, matching the relay's own decode behavior; envelope validation at the port boundary — msg id must be a UUID v4 and recipient a 64-hex blind hash, so PII-shaped values can never reach the wire or a Redis key), `relay_socket.dart` (`RelaySocket`/`RelaySocketConnector` ports + the `hasTransportHeartbeat` capability), `relay_heartbeat.dart` (clock/timer-injectable data-frame watchdog), `relay_client.dart` (orchestration), `dummy_traffic.dart` (opt-in keepalive obfuscation); data layer: `web_socket_relay_socket.dart` (`WebSocketRelaySocket` over `web_socket_channel` ^2.4.0, `WebSocketRelaySocketConnector`)
- **WebSocket client for real-time delivery** — `RelayClient` connects to `GET /v1/relay/ws` and sends the access token + device id in the **FIRST frame body** (`RelayAuthFrame`), never the URL (query strings get logged by proxies — asserted in unit tests AND by the live-socket integration test's server, which checks the upgrade query params are empty). Read loop decodes every inbound frame; `envelope` frames are forwarded on the `envelopes` broadcast stream and **auto-acked** (`RelayAckFrame`), which is what purges the relay's offline Redis Streams queue (Task 4.4). Sender identity is never sent by the client — the relay server overrides `sender_hash`/`sender_device_id` from the authenticated token, so spoofing is structurally impossible
- **Envelope parsing (exact wire parity)** — tests round-trip the EXACT Go protojson shapes (`{"auth":{...}}`, `{"auth_ack":{...}}`, `{"envelope":{...}}`, `{"ack":{...}}`, `{"error":{...}}`) with snake_case fields and base64 ciphertext; malformed/foreign frames are dropped (connection survives bad peers, forward compatibility)
- **Heartbeat/ping-pong for connection health** — production-correct design from code review: the Go relay's 25s pings are **protocol-level control frames** that dart:io auto-pongs and that NEVER surface as data frames, so a data-frame watchdog would falsely tear down healthy idle connections. `WebSocketRelaySocket` instead passes `pingInterval: 20s` to the channel: dart:io sends protocol pings and **closes the connection when the relay stops answering** (dead relay / middlebox kill), which the client observes as a disconnect and reconnects. The `RelayHeartbeat` data-frame watchdog remains for sockets without transport liveness (gated by `RelaySocket.hasTransportHeartbeat` — the real socket reports true, fakes false so tests still exercise the app watchdog)
- **Reconnection logic with exponential backoff** — reuses the Task 5.2 `ExponentialBackoff.delayForRetryWithJitter`; sessions are generation-guarded so stale callbacks (from an old socket after a reconnect) are inert. **Hardened semantics from code review:** a drop DURING the auth handshake before any `auth_ack` arrived is a TRANSIENT transport failure (network blip / relay restart) and reconnects with backoff; only an explicit `auth_ack{authenticated:false}` is terminal (`RelayConnectionPhase.authFailed`), and the relay's subsequent policy-violation close is never mistaken for a transient drop (`_authRejected` flag) — a rejected token is never retried
- **Dummy traffic (opt-in obfuscation)** — `DummyTrafficGenerator` emits scheduled UUID-v4 keepalive envelopes via an injectable `send` callback (clock/timer-injectable); ids are always well-formed UUID v4 — never sequence numbers or anything fingerprintable
- VERIFY PASSED (Flutter): **691 tests total** (655 → **36 new**): wire codec round-trips incl. the EXACT Go protojson shapes + DiscardUnknown + malformed-drop; fake_async heartbeat watchdog (fires on silence, stops cleanly); RelayClient lifecycle — first-frame auth (token in body, URL clean), auth-rejection terminal with no retry, connected transition, inbound envelope auto-ack, pre-wire UUID-v4/blind-hash validation (PII-shaped rejected, nothing invalid hits the wire), post-auth transient reconnect re-authenticates, **pre-auth handshake drop reconnects**, **rejection-close stays terminal**, **transport-heartbeat socket skips the app watchdog (no false stale) yet still reconnects on transport death**, stale-watchdog tear-down/reconnect + activity-resets-window, stop() cancels reconnect, dummy-traffic schedule; real `dart:io` WebSocket server integration — full lifecycle (server asserts empty upgrade query params, first-frame auth, envelope+auto-ack round-trip, client→server envelope, remote-close reconnect re-authenticates) + transport-heartbeat capability pinned; `flutter analyze` **0 issues**, `dart format` clean. Full suite green (2 Argon2id-heavy files — `identity_service_test`, `salt_manager_test` — hang ONLY at the tail of the long serial run, an environment artifact; each passes in isolation, 36 tests)
- **SECURITY CHECKPOINT PASSED:** (1) **JWT in body, never URL** — token exists only in the first auth frame body; unit tests assert the URL carries no token/query and the integration test's server asserts empty upgrade query parameters; (2) **zero PII / no identifiers on the wire** — `sendEnvelope` rejects anything that is not a UUID v4 msg id or 64-hex blind hash BEFORE the bytes leave the device (raw phones/emails/usernames can never reach the wire or a Redis key), sender identity is server-authenticated by construction; (3) **ciphertext never inspected or logged** — the client treats envelopes as opaque bytes (no decrypt, no `print` in `lib/relay`), security-checkpoint suite scans for PII literals + forbidden patterns (a `WebSocket`-ban guard already exists in the sync checkpoints — relay lives in its own tree); (4) **no resource leaks** — `stop()`/`dispose()` cancel the reconnect timer, stop the heartbeat, cancel the frames subscription, and close the socket; generation guards make stale callbacks inert
- **Decisions & notes:** relay is a separate `lib/relay/` tree by design (the sync security checkpoint forbids WebSocket imports in `lib/sync`); `web_socket_channel` ^2.4.0 is the ONLY new dependency (standard Flutter WS transport); transport-level `pingInterval` is the correct liveness mechanism for this protocol (server pings are control frames invisible to data streams) — the app watchdog stays only for sockets without transport heartbeat; post-review hardening covered the two production bugs a fake_async suite cannot catch (false stale-fire on idle, pre-auth drop misclassified as terminal); wiring the relay into the app shell + conversation BLoC (publish/send over the socket) is the Task 6.5+ concern
- **PHASE 6 IN PROGRESS:** Task 6.4 complete — next: Task 6.5 Multi-Device Pairing

### 2026-08-10 — Task 6.3: Message Encryption & Decryption
- **X3DH session establishment wired into the connection-approval hook** (the key exchange deferred from Task 6.2). New `SessionManager` (client `signal/`) orchestrates the full flow: **initiator** establishment runs `X3DHService.initiateX3DH` against the peer's published prekey bundle, **responder** establishment runs `respondToX3DH` with our identity + signed prekey (+ optional one-time prekey), and both initialize a `DoubleRatchetService` stored per-peer in a `SessionStore` port (in-memory impl ships; SQLCipher-backed durable storage is a later Phase-6 concern). `PreKeyBundleSource` port + `InMemoryPreKeyBundleSource` registry (public keys only). The approval hook is a `SessionEstablisher` port: `LocalSessionEstablisher` fetches the requester's bundle, loads/creates the Ed25519 identity from the hardware keystore, and establishes as initiator — **idempotent** (existing sessions reused) and **blind-hash keyed only**. `LocalConnectionRequestsBloc.accept` invokes it **best-effort**: a missing bundle or transient failure NEVER fails the local accept (the request is already committed; a later sync run retries)
- **Composer wiring** — the static 52dp placeholder is gone. New pure-presentational `ConversationComposer` (text field + send button + keyboard action, empty-input disabled, clears after send, zero identifiers/PII). `MessageBloc` gains `send(String)`: `LocalMessageBloc.send` encrypts the plaintext via the `MessageCipher` port (`SignalMessageCipher` over the session manager), persists a `Message(direction: sent, delivered: false)` through `LocalMessageRepository.create` (offline-first — sealed payload enqueued into the encrypted sync queue), then republishes the thread. Send failures (no session) are swallowed at the screen — never an unhandled async exception
- **Message decryption** — `MessageSummary.content` now carries the DECRYPTED plaintext. `LocalMessageBloc` decrypts on every emit (async `_emit` with the same stale-snapshot guard as the 6.2 requests bloc, so an older pull can never overwrite a fresher push). `MessageBubble` renders `content`; undecryptable messages (no session / tampered MAC / ratchet skew) keep `content: null` and show the fixed `[end-to-end encrypted]` placeholder — **never raw ciphertext, never a leak** (a later lost-session decrypt drops stale plaintext from state, proven by test)
- **Explicit direction field** — `MessageDirection { sent, received }` on the `Message` entity; `defaultMessageSide` now reads the persisted direction, **definitively superseding the `delivered ? received : sent` heuristic**. Schema migration **v3** adds `messages.direction` (NOT NULL, backfilled by the old heuristic — `UPDATE ... SET direction='sent' WHERE delivered=0` — so pre-upgrade sent/received rows keep their rendered side); row codecs round-trip the wire name with a conservative `received` fallback for unknown values. `MessageSummary.copyWith` gained a sentinel so content can be explicitly cleared (post-review hardening)
- VERIFY PASSED (Flutter): **655 tests green** (612 → **43 new**: 9 `session_manager_test` establishment/round-trip/isolation/duress-delete; 7 `session_establisher_test` bundle fetch + identity creation + idempotency + `SignalMessageCipher` seal/open/tamper-null; 9 message-bloc encrypt/decrypt (decrypt-on-emit, undecryptable→null, send persists sent/undelivered + queues, no-cipher StateError, content-clear on lost session) + `MessageSummary` content semantics; 3 bubble content rendering (content / received-left / placeholder fallback); 5 `conversation_composer_test`; 3 approval-hook establishment (fires with requester blind hash / no-op without hook / failing establisher never fails accept); direction + schema v3 + migration v3 backfill tests), `flutter analyze` **0 issues**, `dart format` clean
- VERIFY PASSED (Go, unchanged but re-run): **216 test funcs**, `go test -race ./...` green, `gofmt`/`go vet`/`go build` clean, **golangci-lint 0 violations** (exit 0)
- **SECURITY CHECKPOINTS PASSED:** (1) **message content is never logged plaintext** — `lib/state` has zero `print`/`debugPrint`; the only plaintext in state is the decrypted `content` needed for rendering, never the ciphertext; (2) **no ciphertext in BLoC state** — `MessageSummary` exposes only `content`/direction/delivery flags (compile-time guarantee, security-scan suite updated: data-layer blocs may READ entity ciphertext to decrypt, state models still declare none); (3) **zero PII / no 64-hex rendered** — composer + bubbles render no identifiers, `formatPeerHandle` intact, FLAG_SECURE wrappers intact on both screens; (4) **X3DH keyed by blind hash only** — no phone/username in any session key or bundle lookup; tampered ciphertext returns null (placeholder), never raw bytes
- **Decisions & notes:** `MessageCipher`/`SessionEstablisher` are domain ports with signal/data impls so blocs stay decoupled from crypto; `SessionStore` is in-memory (durable SQLCipher-backed session persistence is a follow-up); read-receipt ✓✓✓ and background TTL deletion remain unchecked in the master plan (need the server relay's read-ack + expiry plumbing — later Phase 6 tasks); the v3 backfill preserves pre-6.3 rendering for existing rows
- **PHASE 6 IN PROGRESS:** Task 6.3 complete — next: Task 6.4 WebSocket Message Relay

### 2026-08-09 — Task 6.2: Connection Request Flow
- **Backend: username search via API Gateway** (`services/internal/identity/`) — new **`GET /v1/identity/username/{username}`** route (`routeUsernameLookup`, Go 1.22 `PathValue`) behind `requireAuth` (a 401 without a token — the search surface cannot be scraped anonymously). `Service.LookupUsername` validates the username against the claim policy (invalid formats like `ReKha_K`/`ab`/reserved words NEVER resolve — they return `ErrUsernameNotFound` like unknown names, so no oracle), and a **released username in its 30-day cooldown never resolves** (the cooldown window is a privacy boundary — proven by test). Response is `UsernameLookup{username, blind_hash_id}` only — never a phone, never device info. **5 new Go tests** (found/unknown+invalid/released/over-HTTP 200+404/unauth-401 + no-phone-in-response-and-logs via the redacting logger)
- **Backend: relay wire-contract fix** (`services/internal/relay/requests.go`) — `ConnectionRequest` gained explicit snake_case JSON tags (`id`, `requester_hash`, `recipient_hash`, `status`) so the create-request endpoint emits the same contract as every other endpoint (previously Go-default field names leaked through); the two `server_test.go` raw-key assertions updated to the new contract; `requests_test.go` uses typed structs (verified unaffected)
- **Client domain (`lib/repository/domain/`)** — new `ConnectionRequest` entity + `ConnectionRequestStatus` enum (pending/accepted/rejected/withdrawn with wire names), `ConnectionRequestRepository` port (CRUD + `listIncomingPending`, `findPendingPair`, `send`, `accept`, `reject`, `withdraw`), `UserLookupResult` + `UserSearchRepository` port (the client-side seam for the new lookup endpoint), `UsernameDirectory` port (remembered public username ↔ blind-hash mappings)
- **Client data (`lib/repository/data/`)** — `LocalConnectionRequestRepository` is **local-first with a single-transition CAS state machine mirroring the relay** (only `pending` requests may move; terminal states immutable; `send` is idempotent while pending). **SECURITY: `send` requires a 64-hex blind-hash target — a raw E.164 phone is rejected with `ArgumentError` BEFORE it can reach storage or the sync queue**, and queued envelopes carry ONLY `{action, id, requester_hash, recipient_hash, status}` (the queue cipher additionally seals them at rest). `MemoryUserSearchRepository` + `MemoryUsernameDirectory` are the in-memory seams until the client's network transport lands (same `SyncSink`-port pattern). `sqlite_entity_store.dart` gained the `connection_requests` row codecs (`connectionRequestToRow`/`FromRow` — round-trip tested incl. every wire name)
- **Client state (`lib/state/`)** — `ConnectionRequestsState` + `ConnectionRequestsBloc` (start/refresh/accept/reject/sendRequest) implemented by `LocalConnectionRequestsBloc`: projects the local store into the **UI-safe inbox** (requests targeting the current user still pending), resolves remembered usernames onto `PendingRequestSummary` (now carries `requesterUsername`), and never exposes raw request ids/hashes to widgets. `UserSearchState` + `UserSearchBloc` implemented by `LocalUserSearchBloc` (idle → searching → found/not-found/error). **Post-review hardening:** `_emit` now guards with a monotonic sequence so a stale `refresh()` pull can never overwrite a fresher database push (deterministic race test with a hang-controlled directory), and a throwing directory lookup degrades to the derived handle instead of crashing the inbox stream (both new tests)
- **Client UI (`lib/state/ui/`)** — `VaultConversationListScreen` now consumes the requests BLoC: the Task 6.1 static queue props remain only as the presentation-test fallback; **the inbox is driven by `LocalConnectionRequestsBloc` with Accept wired through** (`_buildList` + `initState` subscription holding the latest state in a field — no nested StreamBuilder, no builder-time refresh loop; the classic late-subscribe case is covered by a dedicated test). Conversation tiles resolve remembered PUBLIC usernames through the `UsernameDirectory` (`@rekha_k` when known, derived `formatPeerHandle` otherwise — the raw 64-hex hash is never rendered). Peers render exclusively via `formatPeerHandle`. FAB + masthead CTA open a new conversation; tapping a row fires `onConversationTap(id)`. **FLAG_SECURE:** the whole screen wraps in `SecureScreenWrapper` (injectable `SecureFlagService` for tests, production default otherwise) and calls `bloc.refresh()` on init (late-subscribe correctness — broadcast stream, no replay)
- **`VaultPendingRequestsSection` (`lib/state/ui/vault_pending_requests_section.dart`)** — collapsible `PENDING REQUESTS (N)` queue section (DESIGN §6.2) with one tile per incoming request: display handle (via `formatPeerHandle`) + Accept button. **Post-review hardening:** the section renders READ-ONLY (no buttons) when the accept flow is not yet wired — the inbox is never invisible; an absent accept handler cannot hide incoming requests

The bubble body is the fixed `[end-to-end encrypted]` placeholder — NEVER decrypted content (Task 6.1 is foundation; decryption is 6.3). `MessageSide` + `MessageSideResolver` (default: undelivered→sent, delivered→received) are injected so the screen stays deterministic and the direction heuristic is replaceable when a real direction field lands

### 2026-08-09 — Task 5.6: Offline Queue Persistence
- **Durable queue persistence across app lifecycles (`lib/repository/data/local_sync_queue_repository.dart`)** — every pending, in-progress, and retryable-failed mutation is durably persisted as a row in the SQLCipher-encrypted database; the row codec (`syncQueueItemToRow`/`syncQueueItemFromRow`) round-trips ALL fields exactly — sealed payload bytes (up to 64 KiB verified), status, retryCount, and microsecond-exact `createdAt`/`lastAttemptAt` (including `null` for never-attempted). The 5 new **exact serialization bounds** tests prove byte-exact payload + microsecond-exact timestamps survive the codec, sub-millisecond epochs are preserved, and unknown status/operation strings throw (strict bounds)
- **Crash recovery & lifecycle safety** — `recoverInterrupted()` (Task 5.2) semantics re-verified at the persistence level: cold-start recovery is covered end-to-end by the new **`queue_persistence_integration_test.dart`** — a queue written by one repository instance is drained/recovered by a fresh instance over a brand-new store (crash-simulation: a second repository instance recovers the stranded `in_progress` item back to `pending` and the worker retries it, never losing or corrupting the sealed payload)
- **Queue size limits — max 1000 items, FIFO eviction** — `LocalSyncQueueRepository.defaultMaxQueueSize = 1000` (master-plan cap) enforced on every insert; when the queue overflows, the OLDEST non-`in_progress` items are evicted first. **In-flight items are never evicted** (a push in progress is protected — bounded overage by the in-flight batch), and — post-review hardening — the **just-inserted item is excluded from eviction candidates BY CONSTRUCTION** (passed by id), so its survival never depends on sort order even when `createdAt` ties with an older item (Dart's `List.sort` is not guaranteed stable; proven by a new tie-timestamp test with a fixed clock)
- **30-day expiry cleanup (`purgeExpired`)** — new `SyncQueueRepository.purgeExpired({now, maxAge = 30 days})` deletes items **strictly older** than the cutoff (an item exactly 30 days old survives — boundary test); it purges across ALL statuses including stranded `in_progress` leftovers, and **`BackgroundSyncWorker.runOnce()` calls it before every drain** so stale mutations past the offline window are never pushed (the server's own 30-day queue TTL would reject them anyway)
- **Queue backup/restore mechanism (`lib/repository/domain/sync_queue_backup.dart`)** — new sealed-only portable envelope:
  - **`serializeQueueBackup`/`deserializeQueueBackup`** — a versioned JSON envelope (`version: 1`, `exportedAt`, ordered oldest-first `items`); deserialization is **fully validated BEFORE any item is returned** (unknown version/operation/status, non-base64 payloads, malformed entries, non-object/non-list structure all throw `FormatException`) so a corrupt backup can never partially mutate a queue
  - **`SyncQueueBackup` store-bound service** — wraps the SAME `EntityStore` the queue repository uses (export reads exactly what is persisted; restore writes exactly what a cold start would read back — no translation layer). **Restore is idempotent**: items whose id is already present are skipped, so restoring the same backup twice (or onto a store holding some of its items) can never duplicate a mutation (post-review hardening + test)
- VERIFY PASSED (unit/integration): **35 new Flutter tests** — **15 queue-repository** (6 size-cap/FIFO incl. the tie-timestamp survival invariant + in-flight protection + survivor-payload integrity, 4 `purgeExpired` incl. the strict-boundary and all-statuses cases + clock default, 5 exact serialization bounds) + **10 backup** (export envelope shape/ordering, byte-exact round-trip into a fresh store with sealed payloads opened by the same key, 6 strict-validation rejections incl. no-partial-restore, idempotent double-restore, PII-free envelope security test) + **2 cold-restart integration** (fresh-instance recovery + worker drain after recovery) + **4 worker expiry-cleanup** (stale purged-before-drain never pushed, exact-30-day boundary retained and pushed, plus boundary companions) + **3 ciphertext-at-rest checkpoint additions** (restart/backup paths) + static security scans. **FULL SUITE: 522 Flutter tests pass** (was 487 → 35 new); **`flutter analyze` → 0 errors**; `dart format` clean (0 files changed); changed trees (repository/sync/state) run **303/303 green**; the Argon2id KDF database suite passes 31/31 in isolation (the occasional full-suite "did not complete" remains the documented CPU-contention flake — every suite passes alone)
- **SECURITY CHECKPOINT PASSED — queued data remains encrypted at rest:** every payload is AES-256-GCM sealed by the `QueuePayloadCipher` BEFORE insertion (the queue store only ever sees ciphertext); the new persistence/backup paths never invoke the cipher (a restore inserts sealed bytes as-is — no double-seal, no decrypt), and the backup envelope carries ONLY sealed payloads + non-PII metadata (UUIDs, operation types, statuses, retry counters, UTC timestamps). Runtime test: exported backup contains zero occurrences of the raw mutation plaintext; static scans prove `sync_queue_backup.dart` contains no `QueuePayloadCipher` reference and the worker's expiry path deletes rows without touching payload bytes
- **Post-review hardening (code-reviewer findings, all fixed + re-verified):** FIFO eviction no longer relies on sort stability — the just-inserted item is excluded by id so a `createdAt` tie can never evict it (new tie-timestamp test); `restoreQueue` is now idempotent (skips present ids — no duplicates on double-restore, new test); the exact-30-day boundary semantics of `purgeExpired` were confirmed covered by tests; backup/restore round-trip was verified byte-exact for sealed payloads and microsecond timestamps
- **Decisions & notes:** restore is a disaster-recovery flow targeting a fresh/empty encrypted store (double-restore and partial-overlap are safe no-ops by the idempotency skip); `purgeExpired` deliberately runs before the drain in the worker (cheap single read sweep per cycle — the encrypted queue is bounded by the 1000-item cap anyway); backup version 1 is validated strictly so a future format change requires an explicit codec migration; the worker calls `purgeExpired` and `recoverInterrupted` in the same pre-drain step, keeping crash-recovery and retention enforcement in one place
  - **PHASE 5 COMPLETE (5.1–5.6).** Next: Phase 6 Task 6.1 Vault UI Foundation

### 2026-08-05 — Task 5.5: Conflict Resolution Logic
- **Conflict-resolution policy refined (`lib/repository/domain/conflict_resolution.dart`)** — the Task 5.2 baseline `ServerAuthoritativeLastWriteWins` is now a complete deterministic engine:
  - **`MutationVersion`** gained an optional numeric `value` (non-PII aggregate payload, e.g. karma) alongside the existing `(serverAcknowledged, timestamp, authorHash)`; the `ConflictResolution` outcome now also carries a **`mergedValue`** for merge paths
  - **Rule 1 (authority):** a server-acknowledged version beats a local-only unacknowledged version — absolute, regardless of timestamp (the server's commit is canonical); **Rule 2 (recency):** among versions of equivalent authority the higher timestamp wins; **Rule 3 (deterministic tiebreak):** exact timestamp ties break on `authorHash` (the 64-hex blind identifier) so every peer device converges on the SAME winner — no split-brain (byte-identical comparison)
  - **Merge logic for nested aggregates** — new `MergePolicy` port + **`MaxMergePolicy`** (commutative, idempotent, associative — CRDT-compatible) for numeric aggregates like karma scores; **`MergeAwareLastWriteWins`** composes authority/recency/tiebreak on the entity shell with the merge policy on the value, so both sides of a conflict contribute (e.g., a local offline karma gain merged with the server's concurrent gain rather than one clobbering the other)
- **Sync port evolved to carry conflicts (`lib/repository/domain/sync_sink.dart`)** — `SyncSink.push` now returns a **`SyncPushOutcome`** (`acknowledged` | `rejected` | `conflict` carrying the server's `MutationVersion`) instead of a bare `bool` — the worker and both repositories now see server-side divergence (Task 5.3 409s, replayed responses, pull-merge state) instead of a lossy yes/no
- **New `SyncConflictResolver` (`lib/repository/domain/sync_conflict_resolver.dart`)** — maps a push outcome to a deterministic **disposition**: `success` (acked) / `retry` (rejected, or local-wins conflict via an injected non-server-authoritative policy — the extension point) / `superseded` (server-acknowledged divergence — the queued mutation is dropped, entity state converges on the next pull). The worker applies them: `success` → ack+delete, `superseded` → delete (no retry — the server's version is canonical), `retry` → markFailed with the existing jittered backoff; `SyncResult` gained a `conflicts` counter
- **Sync integration (worker + repositories)** — `BackgroundSyncWorker.runOnce()` and the `LocalMessageRepository` / `LocalConversationRepository` sync loops all route pushes through the resolver; an `applyLocal` hook lets a repository supply the authoritative local `MutationVersion` for aggregate merges, so the merge path is genuinely reachable through the sync loop (proven by tests, not dead code)
- **User-facing conflict UI (`lib/state/ui/conflict_resolution_banner.dart`)** — `ConflictResolutionBanner` renders the outcome of a resolution using fixed, hardcoded non-PII labels (`"Changes merged"`, `"Server version kept"`, `"Local change pending retry"`, `"Conflicts resolved: N"`); a reusable component ready for app-shell wiring (the repo has no app shell yet — same delivery pattern as `SyncStatusBar` in Task 5.4)
- VERIFY PASSED (unit): **40 new Flutter tests** — 13 new conflict-resolution domain tests (merge policies, **multi-device convergence**: 3 simulated devices with scripted clocks all pick the identical winner via the author-hash tiebreak; out-of-order mutations, server-vs-local authority, equivalent-authority recency, exact-tie determinism; total file 21) + **7 resolver tests** (acked→success, rejected→retry, server-conflict→superseded under the default policy, local-wins→retry via an injected timestamp-first policy, merge disposition with mergedValue, superseded deletion) + **5 worker integration tests** (a 409-carrying sink's conflict → item deleted + `conflicts` counted; mixed batch of acked + conflicted + retried items → partial progress; server-authoritative supersede removes a divergent local edit) + **repository integration tests** (`local_first_test.dart`: conflicting push outcomes resolved inside the repo sync loop) + **6 banner widget tests** + **7 security scans**. **FULL SUITE: 487 Flutter tests pass** (was 447 → 40 new); **`flutter analyze` → 0 errors**; `dart format` clean; the changed trees (repository/sync/state) run **268/268 green**; the Argon2id KDF database suite passes 31/31 in isolation (the occasional full-suite "did not complete" remains the documented CPU-contention flake — every suite passes alone)
- **SECURITY CHECKPOINT PASSED:** conflict routines operate exclusively on `MutationVersion` (boolean + UTC timestamp + 64-hex `authorHash`), UUIDs, and encrypted/sealed payloads — zero raw PII or decrypted payloads in any comparison, merge, or disposition path; static source scans of `conflict_resolution.dart`, `sync_conflict_resolver.dart`, `sync_sink.dart`, and the banner prove no phone/email/token/PII-shaped literals and no `print(`/`debugPrint(`; the banner renders only the fixed non-PII label strings (runtime widget-tree scan); nothing user-generated is ever logged or rendered during resolution
- **Post-review hardening (code-reviewer findings, all fixed + re-verified):** `SyncConflictResolver` moved from `lib/sync/domain/` to `lib/repository/domain/` (it depends only on repository-domain types — `lib/sync` must not import repository internals; all importers updated, 73 targeted tests re-run green); the merge path is now genuinely reachable via the `applyLocal` local-version hook (was construction-only); the banner is a presentational outcome component with app-shell wiring deferred to Phase 6 (documented, matching the `SyncStatusBar` precedent)
- **Decisions & notes:** the default policy is strictly server-authoritative — every conflict with a server-acknowledged remote resolves to `superseded` by design (Rule 1); `retry` is the tested extension point for injected non-server-authoritative policies; `MaxMergePolicy` is idempotent + commutative + associative so a merged aggregate can never diverge across devices regardless of merge order; `MutationVersion.value` is deliberately numeric-only (non-PII aggregate), preserving the privacy invariant
  - **PHASE 5 IN PROGRESS:** Tasks 5.1–5.5 complete — next: Task 5.6 Offline Queue Persistence

Note: the real-Argon2id suites occasionally report "did not complete" only when the machine is heavily loaded (pre-existing environmental flakiness — every suite passes in isolation and the full run is green with `--concurrency=1`)

### 2026-08-05 — Task 5.3: Idempotency Enforcement (server-side Redis dedup)
- **New domain package `internal/idempotency`** — production idempotency for the relay's mutation endpoints, built on the existing Redis Sentinel HA client (Task 4.6):
  - `store.go` — `RedisStore` over the `cache.Client` with the full lifecycle: **Claim** (atomic `SET NX` of an `in_progress` entry — exactly one concurrent request wins), **Get** (read a stored outcome), **Complete** (persist the finished 2xx response + refresh TTL), **Clear** (delete the key so a failed attempt's retry reprocesses). All values JSON-encoded `{status, body, content_type}`; interface behind it keeps the middleware testable against miniredis
  - `middleware.go` — `Middleware` (`Wrap(next)`) applied **inside** the relay's auth middleware so every POST mutation (create-request, withdraw) dedupes per authenticated actor: **missing `Idempotency-Key` → passthrough** (backwards compatible), **malformed/non-UUID-v4 → 400**, **claim-winner → handler runs**, **concurrent in-flight (lost the SETNX) → 409 Conflict**, **completed → replay cached response** with an `Idempotent-Replayed` header, **handler failure or non-2xx → key cleared** (next retry reprocesses). Responses are captured via a bounded recorder (64 KiB cap) that streams through to the client while buffering for replay — oversized bodies fall back to non-cached execution rather than memory blowup
  - **Actor scoping (security posture):** keys are `idempotency:{blind_hash_id}:{uuid}` via `cache.IdempotencyKeyScoped` — user B replaying user A's UUID can NEVER receive A's cached response; dedup is strictly per-actor
- **Namespace registry (`internal/cache/namespaces.go`)** — new `idempotency` namespace with `IdempotencyTTL = 24h` (spec) and a **strict RFC 4122 UUID v4 validator** (`uuidV4Re`: lowercase hex, version nibble `4`, variant `[89ab]`) reused by both key builders. The builder REJECTS any PII-shaped suffix before a Redis write — raw E.164 phones, raw OTPs, emails, non-UUID strings can never become idempotency keys (Security Checkpoint invariant maintained)
- **Config** — new `IDEMPOTENCY_TTL` (default 24h, parsed + validated; test covers default and override)
- **Wiring** — `cmd/relay/main.go` constructs the `RedisStore` + middleware (using the relay's existing cache client) and passes it via `ServerOptions`; `internal/relay/server.go` wraps the POST mutation handlers inside the auth middleware (401-before-dedup verified — unauthenticated requests never touch the dedup namespace)
- VERIFY PASSED (unit): **18 new idempotency tests** — store lifecycle (claim winner/loser, complete, clear, TTL applied on claim+complete), middleware (missing→passthrough, malformed→400, replay of cached 2xx + `Idempotent-Replayed` header, failure→clear, silent-2xx-not-cached, oversized-body→not-cached, **concurrent-race single-side-effect via miniredis**, **TTL expiry via miniredis FastForward**, **cross-actor no-dedup**, PII-key rejection) + **5 new relay HTTP integration tests** (`server_idempotency_test.go`, real server + auth): duplicate-key single create, replay after completion, 409 on concurrent, cross-actor isolation, malformed 400. **Total: 211 Go test funcs** — full `go test -race -count=1 ./...` green (11 suites), `golangci-lint run` → **0 violations (exit 0)**, gofmt/vet/build clean, `verify_go_deps.sh` checkpoint passed
- VERIFY PASSED (live): relay boots with the dedup stack (Redis + Postgres + NATS), auth correctly precedes the middleware (no-token mutation → 401, no Redis write); the middleware paths (passthrough/400/409/replay/cross-actor) are exercised end-to-end through the real HTTP server in the integration tests
- **SECURITY CHECKPOINT PASSED:** idempotency keys are RFC 4122 UUID v4 from `Random.secure()` (client, Task 5.2) — unpredictable; server-side the key builder strictly validates UUID v4 shape and rejects PII-shaped suffixes before any Redis write; keys are actor-scoped blind-hash + UUID only (no phones, no raw data, no cross-user replay); cached responses are the actor's own, never another user's
- **Decisions & notes:** middleware lives in `internal/idempotency` (not inline in server.go) per the clean-architecture hand-off; the 409-wait-state follows standard REST practice (client retries after backoff — Task 5.2's jittered retry is the natural fit); TTL of 24h comfortably exceeds the client's retry window (5 min max backoff) and matches the plan; `Idempotent-Replayed` header gives clients observability into dedup; the relay's existing 30-day connection-request create is now ALSO idempotency-key protected (an accidental double-create with the same key yields the cached 201, not a duplicate)
  - **PHASE 5 IN PROGRESS:** Tasks 5.1–5.4 complete — next: Task 5.5 Conflict Resolution Logic

### 2026-08-05 — Runtime Diagnosis & Startup Fixes (services fail to boot out of the box)
- **Symptom:** following the documented dev run (README / plain `go run ./cmd/...`) the services crash before serving a single request; root causes reproduced live and fixed:
  - **`go run ./cmd/api` failed instantly** — `config.FromEnv()` unconditionally required `POSTGRES_DSN` even though the API scaffold has no database dependency. FIX: new `APP_REQUIRE_POSTGRES` opt-out (default `true`, identity/relay keep the hard requirement) + `cmd/api` sets `APP_REQUIRE_POSTGRES=false`; new config test covers the opt-out and the preserved default
  - **README dev commands pointed at the wrong database** — they used `localhost:5432`, which on this machine is an UNRELATED project's PostgreSQL. The civic container publishes on **5433**. Following the docs verbatim produced `pq: password authentication failed` (reproduced). FIX: README dev-run sections rewritten with the correct civic DSN (`postgres://civic_app:civic_app_dev@localhost:5433/civic_commons?sslmode=disable`), correct Redis port (6381 — 6379 is another project's instance), and `PG_ENC_KEY`
  - **Relay defaulted to port 8080 too** — identity AND relay both defaulted to `:8080`, so starting both together (as the README implies) collides on the second bind. FIX: `cmd/relay` now defaults `HTTP_PORT=8081` and `SERVICE_NAME=relay` when unset (identity defaults `SERVICE_NAME=identity`); README already documented 8081 — the code now matches
  - **`civic_app` role could not log in** — migration 0001 creates it `NOLOGIN` (secrets activation is documented as a cluster-bootstrap step), so a naive dev run silently fell back to the `postgres` superuser, which **BYPASSES RLS** (defeats the Task 4.5 checkpoint). FIX: `scripts/dev_up.sh` activates the role with a dev password + schema USAGE
  - **NEW crash found by running as `civic_app`:** the migration runner's `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS schema_migrations` fails with `permission denied for schema public` — PostgreSQL 15+ does not grant `CREATE` on the `public` schema by default, and the services run the runner at EVERY startup (not just first boot). FIX: `dev_up.sh` grants `USAGE, CREATE ON SCHEMA public` + `SELECT, INSERT, DELETE ON schema_migrations` to `civic_app` (both dev and any real bootstrap need this — documented in-script)
  - **Fresh-DB bootstrap was missing from the run path** — extension creation (postgis/pgcrypto) needs superuser, so a brand-new container could never be migrated by the service role. FIX: `dev_up.sh` applies the embedded `0001_init.up.sql` + version record transactionally as the `postgres` bootstrap role (psql `-1`) BEFORE activating `civic_app`; the Go runner then sees version 1 recorded and skips it
  - **Half-set dev fallbacks gave a cryptic error** — setting only `IDENTITY_DEV_SALT_HEX` (without `IDENTITY_DEV_JWT_KEY`) fell through to `jwt: no PEM block`. FIX: `resolveSecrets` now fails fast naming the exact missing variable
  - **New file:** `scripts/dev_up.sh` — brings up civic-dedicated containers (postgis:16 on 5433, redis:7 on 6381, nats on 4222 — never reusing other projects' 6379/5432), applies migrations as bootstrap, activates the RLS role, and prints the full eval-able env block (`--env`)
- VERIFY PASSED (live): identity started as `civic_app` against BOTH the existing DB and a freshly-created scratch DB → `/v1/identity/me` 401, `otp/request` valid phone **200** + blind_hash_id, invalid phone **400**, **zero phone digits in the logs**; relay started on **:8081** with JetStream connected (`CIVIC_EVENTS` stream created) → `/v1/relay/requests` 401; **RLS behavior proven live**: a separate `outsider` role granted SELECT on `users` sees **0 rows** while `civic_app` sees its own data; `go test -race ./...` → all 10 suites green (~190 tests); `golangci-lint run` → **0 violations (exit 0)**; gofmt/vet/build clean

### 2026-08-04 — Tasks 5.1 & 5.2: Network State Detection + Sync Worker / Offline Mutation Queue
- **Completed Phase 5.1: Network State Detection** (finalized — most of the layer was built in Task 3.4; this task closed the plan's remaining checkbox)

  - **Existing 5.1 pieces confirmed in place (Task 3.4):** `NetworkInfoProvider` port + `ConnectivityNetworkInfoProvider` (connectivity_plus data impl: none→offline, mobile→metered, wifi/ethernet/vpn/bluetooth/other→online); `NetworkStatus { online, offline, metered }`; `ReconnectionSyncTrigger` (fires exactly once per offline→online transition, never while offline, crash-safe fire-and-forget)
  - VERIFY PASSED (unit): **5 new debounce tests** via `fake_async` (transitive from flutter_test) — window boundary (fires at exactly 500ms, not at 499ms), **flapping collapse** (4 rapid changes → 1 trailing emission of the last status), **window restart** (a change at t=400ms restarts the window), custom duration honored, `currentStatus` passthrough; plus the existing mapping + scripted-transition + trigger tests. **SECURITY CHECKPOINT:** debouncing is pure timing — no network state ever leaves the device, never persisted, never combined with user data (static checkpoint suite already forbids sqflite/secure_storage in lib/sync)
  - **Also fixed:** the pre-existing analyzer warning in `reconnection_sync_trigger.dart` (`catchError` handler must return a `SyncResult` — body_might_complete_normally) — now returns `SyncResult(pushed: 0, failed: 0)` and imports `local_first_repository.dart`
- **Completed Phase 5.2: Sync Worker Implementation / Offline Mutation Queue & Local Persistence** (hand-off scope: queue architecture, idempotency & replay, conflict-resolution hooks, privacy & encryption)
  - **Encrypted local transactional queue** — confirmed + extended from Phase 3: `sync_queue` table in the SQLCipher-encrypted database, every payload sealed with **AES-256-GCM** (`AesGcmQueuePayloadCipher`, Argon2id-derived 256-bit key) BEFORE storage — the queue persists only ciphertext (SECURITY CHECKPOINT maintained; queue_encryption_checkpoint_test suite still green)
  - **Idempotency & replay framing** — new `lib/sync/domain/idempotency_key.dart`: `IdempotencyKeyGenerator` produces **RFC 4122 UUID v4** keys from `Random.secure()` (crypto-random, unpredictable), exposing the standard `Idempotency-Key` header name. **Every queued mutation's id is now a UUID v4 idempotency key** (`LocalSyncQueueRepository._nextId` replaced the `q-<ts>-<n>` scheme) — the transport attaches `Idempotency-Key: <item.id>` so server-side dedup makes retries safe
  - **Deterministic retry with exponential backoff + jitter (WIRED, not dead code)** — code-review finding fixed: retry/backoff now has real production callers. `ExponentialBackoff.delayForRetry` stays the deterministic 1s→2s→4s→…→5min schedule; new `delayForRetryWithJitter(int, {Random?})` applies **equal jitter over [base/2, base)** — a guaranteed minimum (safe as a retry GATE) while still decorrelating a fleet of reconnecting devices (thundering-herd avoidance). **Schema v2 migration** adds `sync_queue.last_attempt_at` (Task 5.2), stamped by `markInProgress`; new `SyncQueueRepository.getRetryable({now, retryDelay})` returns failed items whose backoff window has elapsed; `BackgroundSyncWorker.runOnce()` drains **pending + retryable-failed** items together — a failed item is genuinely retried after its jittered backoff (never before base/2, always by base), and `retryCount` growth extends the window (1s → 2s → …). Deterministic boundary tests use an injectable clock
  - **Crash recovery & local persistence** — new `SyncQueueRepository.recoverInterrupted()`: items stranded `in_progress` by a killed run (crash, battery pull, OS kill) are reset to `pending`; `BackgroundSyncWorker.runOnce()` calls it FIRST so every drain retries interrupted work — nothing is lost; sealed payloads untouched (recovery flips status only)
  - **Aggressive 10s timeout per request** — the worker wraps every `SyncSink.push` in a `Future.timeout` (default **10s** per the master plan; configurable); a hung connection is treated as a failure (markFailed → retryCount++), never stalling the drain
  - **Deterministic conflict-resolution hooks** — new `lib/repository/domain/conflict_resolution.dart`: `ConflictResolutionPolicy` port + default **`ServerAuthoritativeLastWriteWins`** — (1) a server-acknowledged version beats a local-only version, (2) newer timestamp wins, (3) exact ties break deterministically on `authorHash` so every device converges on the SAME winner (no split-brain). Full user-facing conflict UI deferred to Task 5.5
  - **Silent failure handling** — confirmed: thrown/timeout sink errors never propagate to the UI; failed items increment retryCount and stay in the encrypted queue for the next reconnection; reconnection trigger catches async errors (crash-safe)
  - VERIFY PASSED (unit): **35 new Flutter tests** — 8 idempotency (UUID shape/version nibble/variant, ×1000 uniqueness, seeded-RNG determinism, header constant) + 5 debounce + 4 backoff-jitter + 4 retry-eligibility (not-before-window / retried-after-window / backoff-growth / jitter-decorrelation via injectable clock) + 3 crash-recovery + 2 timeout + 8 conflict-resolution + 1 queue-id-UUID-v4 + 1 migration-v1→v2 upgrade + trigger-fix + state-fake updates. **Full suite: 414 tests pass** (379 baseline + 35 new); `dart analyze` → **0 errors** (46 pre-existing info-level nits only); `dart format` clean. Note: the real-Argon2id suites (crypto_service, wrong_key_failure, database_key_service, identity_service, database security_checkpoint) intermittently report "did not complete" when the machine is under load (load avg ~5 from the dev client + dart processes) — every one passes in isolation, pre-existing environmental flakiness unrelated to these changes
  - **SECURITY CHECKPOINT PASSED:** queue payloads remain AES-256-GCM sealed at rest (recovery/timeout/idempotency paths never re-encrypt or expose plaintext); idempotency keys are UUID v4 from a crypto-secure RNG (unpredictable); conflict hooks carry hash-only identifiers (never PII); sync worker still performs zero direct HTTP (sink-only) and never logs payloads — static + runtime checkpoint suites green
  - **Files touched:** `client/lib/sync/domain/debounced_network_info_provider.dart` (new), `client/lib/repository/domain/{idempotency_key,conflict_resolution}.dart` (new), `client/lib/repository/domain/{sync_queue_item,sync_queue_repository,exponential_backoff}.dart`, `client/lib/repository/data/{local_sync_queue_repository,sqlite_entity_store}.dart`, `client/lib/database/domain/{schema,migration}.dart` (v2), `client/lib/sync/data/background_sync_worker.dart`, `client/lib/sync/domain/reconnection_sync_trigger.dart`, `client/pubspec.yaml` (+fake_async dev-dep), `client/test/{sync,repository,database}/…` (+state/fakes.dart for the new interface methods)

### 2026-08-04 — Task 4.8: HashiCorp Vault Integration
- **Completed Phase 4.8: HashiCorp Vault Integration (final task of Phase 4)**
  - **Expanded Vault client** (`internal/vault/vault.go`, still **stdlib net/http, no SDK**): the Task 4.3 static-token KV reader is now a full client — `ReadKV2` + **`ReadKV2Meta`** (version/created_time for rotation awareness); **AppRole authentication** (`LoginAppRole`: `role_id` + `secret_id` → client token, 403 → `ErrPermissionDenied` sentinel); **token lifecycle** (`LookupSelf` TTL/renewable, `RenewSelf`, and a **background `RunRenewal` loop** that re-logs-in and re-renews on a configurable interval, restarting on failure — no more static-token-only operation); **Transit engine helpers** (`TransitEncrypt`/`TransitDecrypt` — base64 plaintext per the Vault transit API, returns `vault:v1:` ciphertext) for key-derivation/encryption needs; **`SecretCache`** (TTL-aware cached reads with a rotation-detecting `Refresh` that reports `changed` and wipes the superseded copy); **`WipeBytes`** zero-memory hygiene helper; and a **`Connect` factory** (static token OR AppRole, `RenewInterval` → background renewal, fail-fast on missing credentials)
  - **Service integration:** `cmd/identity`'s `resolveSecrets` and `cmd/relay`'s `resolvePublicKey` now build the client through `vault.Connect` — **fail-fast in production** (a missing Vault addr/token/role makes the service refuse to start; dev fallbacks remain strictly non-production via config enforcement)
  - **Config additions:** `VAULT_ROLE_ID`, `VAULT_SECRET_ID`, `VAULT_RENEW_INTERVAL` (default 5m) + a **production rule: non-dev environments require Vault auth** (token or AppRole creds) — new `TestFromEnvRequiresVaultAuthInProduction`
  - **Logging redaction for Vault secrets** (`internal/logging`): the redactor now intercepts **Vault token shapes** (`hvs.`/`s.` prefixed tokens), **`X-Vault-Token:` request headers**, **`Authorization: Bearer <vault-token>`**, and generic `X-Vault-*` request headers → `[REDACTED]` — so a stray token/header dumped into a log attribute can never leak (unit-tested)
  - VERIFY PASSED (unit): **20 new tests** — AppRole login success/reject/missing-creds, `LookupSelf`/`RenewSelf` + renewal-loop firing (background loop verified against a scriptable httptest Vault), `Connect` (AppRole + static token + requires-credentials), KV v2 + metadata read, transit round-trip + base64-encoding, **SecretCache TTL** (served from memory until expiry, then re-fetched) + **rotation detection** (rotated value picked up and reported changed), HTTP **401/403/404/500 sentinel mapping**, `WipeBytes`, redaction of `hvs.` tokens / `X-Vault-Token` / Bearer headers, config production-enforcement. **190 total Go tests**, `go test -race ./...` green (full suite under race), gofmt/vet/build clean, **golangci-lint run → 0 violations (exit 0)**, `scripts/verify_go_deps.sh` → SECURITY CHECKPOINT PASSED
  - VERIFY PASSED (live, `scripts/verify_vault_live.sh` — real `hashicorp/vault:1.15.6` dev server in docker with a `civic-commons` kv-v2 mount + transit engine + an AppRole role): **ALL checks passed** — Vault container ready; KV v2 salt + JWT public key written and read back through the real API; **AppRole login → authenticated KV read → `RenewSelf`** against the real server; **transit encrypt/decrypt round-trip**; `SecretCache` fetch + refresh; **wrong token rejected with the 403 sentinel** (fail-fast auth); redaction/config security tests re-run live
  - **SECURITY CHECKPOINT PASSED — secrets are never written to application logs:** the redacting slog handler + string redactor scrub Vault token shapes and auth headers before any sink (proven by unit tests at the redaction boundary and re-checked in the live script); service config in production **requires** Vault auth (no silent unauthenticated fallbacks); secret buffers are wiped on cache replacement / via `WipeBytes`; Vault tokens never appear in subjects, log attributes, or error strings (redactor applied at the handler boundary)
  - **FIXED during development:** Vault dev server refuses to start with an `hvs.`-prefixed `-dev-root-token-id` (reserved prefix — verified empirically) → the live script uses a plain token id; kv-v2 secrets must be written with `vault kv put` (plain `vault write` hits the wrong path — 404) → script corrected; the `verify_vault_live.sh` redaction check suffered the same `pipefail` + `grep -q` SIGPIPE issue as the NATS script → captured to a file first; errcheck flagged the unchecked type-assertion inside a test handler's `Write` call (`check-type-assertions: true`) → hoisted to a comma-ok local; the correct server image is `hashicorp/vault:1.15` (the `vault:` shorthand is the CLI image)

### 2026-08-04 — Task 4.7: NATS JetStream Event Bus
- **Completed Phase 4.7: NATS JetStream Event Bus**
  - **JetStream client** (`internal/events/nats.go`, rewritten from the Task 4.1 factory): `events.Options` (URL, stream name, storage, max age, reconnect budget/wait/timeout, redacting logger) + `NewClient` dials with **auto-reconnect handlers** (disconnect warn, reconnect info w/ url, closed-with-last-error; nil-safe for clean disconnects). `EnsureStream` idempotently creates/reconciles the **CIVIC_EVENTS** stream (File storage, LimitsPolicy, MaxAge, DiscardOld) on every startup; `Publish` waits for the JetStream **PUBACK** (at-least-once — an error means not committed, safe to retry); `SubscribeDurable` creates an explicit durable consumer then **BINDS** to it — the nats.go bind path never marks the consumer for deletion, so ack progress survives drains/restarts (a durable created implicitly by `js.Subscribe` is deleted on Unsubscribe/Drain — verified empirically and fixed); handler errors **NAK** for redelivery; `Flush`/`Close`/`Connected` helpers
  - **Validated topic schema** (`internal/events/subjects.go`): allowlist registry — `relay.connection.accepted`, `identity.user.registered`, `karma.updated`, `search.sync.requested` — plus `DefaultStreamConfig()` capturing all four with 30-day retention. **SECURITY CHECKPOINT — zero plaintext PII:** `ValidateSubject` enforces the allowlist AND rejects any E.164 phone / e-mail anywhere in the subject; `ValidatePayload` rejects PII-carrying bodies (reusing a new exported `logging.ContainsPII`); unregistered subjects are refused before the wire
  - **At-least-once guarantees:** publishers get a PUBACK or an error; durable consumers with explicit acks resume after crashes and NAK-handler-error redelivers; nats.go reconnect (configurable budget, default infinite) with logged handlers covers broker restarts
  - **Config additions:** `NATS_URL`, `NATS_STREAM_NAME` (default CIVIC_EVENTS), `NATS_STORAGE` (file|memory), `NATS_MAX_AGE` (30d), `NATS_MAX_RECONNECTS` (-1 = infinite), `NATS_RECONNECT_WAIT` (2s), `NATS_CONNECT_TIMEOUT` (5s) — all with defaults + override tests
  - **Relay wiring** (`cmd/relay/main.go`): the ad-hoc core-NATS `natsPublisher` is gone — the relay now publishes connection-accepted events through the JetStream client with stream init at startup, keeping the `relay.NoopEventPublisher` dev fallback when NATS/stream init fails
  - VERIFY PASSED (unit): **11 new events tests** against a real **in-process nats-server v2.10.24** (test-only dep, same pattern as miniredis; go directive stays pinned at **1.22.0** after re-pinning transitive jwt/v2 + nkeys + x/crypto + klauspost): stream creation idempotency + subject set + File storage; pub/sub round-trip through a durable consumer; **NAK redelivery** (first attempt fails → second succeeds); **restart-resume** — events published before a server restart on the same store dir are redelivered and ACKED events are NOT replayed (no event loss, verified by consumer state); **concurrent durable-create race** (two subscribers creating the same durable both succeed — `ErrConsumerNameAlreadyInUse` is benign); **Options storage/maxage fallback** (NATS_STORAGE/NATS_MAX_AGE take effect on the stream even when a custom StreamConfig is passed); subject allowlist + PII rejection (phone/email subjects, phone/email payloads, unregistered subjects); payload PII rejection; plus 2 config tests. **170 total Go tests**, `go test -race` green (events/config/logging), gofmt/vet/build clean, **golangci-lint run → 0 violations (exit 0)**, dependency checkpoint passed
  - VERIFY PASSED (live, `scripts/verify_nats_live.sh` — real `nats:2.10-alpine` container with `-js` file storage on a docker volume): **8/8 checks** — JetStream enabled; CIVIC_EVENTS stream created with File storage; live pub/sub through a durable consumer (5 events acked); **DURABILITY: container stopped + restarted → the CIVIC_EVENTS stream survived and the durable consumer still round-trips (no event loss)**; PII-shaped subjects/payloads rejected
  - **SECURITY CHECKPOINT PASSED:** event subjects are an **allowlist** (only the 4 registered non-PII subjects) and both subjects + payloads are rejected if they contain E.164 phones or e-mails — proven by unit tests at the validation point; reconnect/closed handlers log through the redacting logger; the chart carries no credentials
  - **Post-review hardening (code-reviewer findings, all fixed + re-verified):** `Options.Storage`/`Options.MaxAge` were dead config (the client stored but never read them — `NATS_STORAGE`/`NATS_MAX_AGE` had no effect) → `EnsureStream` now falls back to the Options values when the passed `StreamConfig` leaves them zero, and the relay passes cfg-driven storage/maxage explicitly (proven by a new test); a concurrent `AddConsumer` race (two replicas/consumer-group members creating the same durable at once) now treats `ErrConsumerNameAlreadyInUse` as benign and proceeds to bind (new race test); the MASTER_PLAN changelog v1.7 row was inserted before v1.6 → reordered; `MaxReconnects` zero-value semantics documented (-1 = infinite prod default)

### 2026-08-04 — Task 4.6: Redis Configuration
- **Completed Phase 4.6: Redis Configuration**
  - **Production client factory** (`internal/cache/redis.go`): `cache.Options` + `NewClient` builds a **Sentinel HA failover client** (`redis.NewFailoverClient`) when `REDIS_SENTINEL_ADDRS` is set, else a standalone client — both returning the same `*redis.Client` so every store is unchanged. Production-safe defaults: pool size 20, min-idle 5, **3 retries** with 8ms→512ms backoff (resilient through failover windows), 5s/3s/3s dial/read/write timeouts, 4s pool timeout, 5m conn max-idle. A `cache.Ping(ctx, client)` health-check probe is run at identity + relay startup (logged, non-fatal — lazy client recovers on reconnect) and both mains now `Close()` the client on shutdown
  - **Validated namespace registry** (`internal/cache/namespaces.go`): all techstack §7.2 namespaces — `otp`, `otp_attempts`, `refresh`, `revoked`, `revoked_family`, `msg_queue`, `karma`, `vote_buffer`, `analyst_load`, `rate` — with spec TTL policy constants (10m OTP/attempts, 30d refresh/revoked/revoked_family/msg_queue, 5m karma, 1m rate, no-TTL vote_buffer/analyst_load). **Key builders validate the suffix before building**: a key may only carry a 64-hex blind_hash_id, a 64-hex SHA-256 digest, or a 32-hex family id
  - **SECURITY CHECKPOINT — no plaintext PII in Redis keys/values:** the identity OTP store (`otp:`/`otp_attempts:`), refresh store (`refresh:`/`revoked:`/`revoked_family:`) and relay offline queue (`msg_queue:`) now build every key through the validated builders, so **a raw E.164 phone number, raw OTP code, or any free-form string can never become a Redis key**. Tests prove rejection for PII-shaped suffixes (`+919876543210`, `14155552671`, `user@example.com`, short/non-hex/uppercase ids); the existing runtime test still proves OTP codes are stored bcrypt-hashed and phones never appear in the miniredis dump
  - **Redis Sentinel Helm chart** (`infrastructure/helm/redis/`, expanded from the 1-replica stub): `redis` StatefulSet (pod ordinal 0 = primary, ordinals 1..N follow via `replicaof redis-0.redis-headless`) + `redis-sentinel` StatefulSet (3 nodes, quorum 2) + headless/sentinel services; **AOF + RDB persistence** (`appendonly yes`, `appendfsync everysec`, RDB save points) on a PVC, `maxmemory-policy noeviction` so queued messages/tokens/OTPs are never evicted; password via `existingSecret` (ESO/Vault) — never in the chart
  - **Config additions** (`internal/config`): `REDIS_SENTINEL_ADDRS` (comma-separated), `REDIS_SENTINEL_MASTER` (default civic-master), `REDIS_SENTINEL_PASSWORD`, and tuning knobs `REDIS_POOL_SIZE`/`REDIS_MIN_IDLE_CONNS`/`REDIS_MAX_RETRIES`/`REDIS_MIN_RETRY_BACKOFF`/`REDIS_MAX_RETRY_BACKOFF`/`REDIS_DIAL_TIMEOUT`/`REDIS_READ_TIMEOUT`/`REDIS_WRITE_TIMEOUT`/`REDIS_POOL_TIMEOUT` — all with defaults + override tests
  - VERIFY PASSED (unit): **19 new tests** — namespace prefix/TTL spec conformance, key builders produce exact spec keys, **PII-shaped suffix rejection** across every builder (incl. a relay-level guard: `Enqueue`/`Len` reject raw-phone-shaped recipients and create no junk keys), family-id/endpoint validation, client defaults + overrides, standalone basic ops, **TTL expiration via miniredis FastForward**, stream **XTRIM MINID age eviction + drain purge**. **152 total Go tests**, `go test -race ./...` green, gofmt/vet/build clean, **golangci-lint run → 0 violations (exit 0)**
  - VERIFY PASSED (live, `scripts/verify_redis_live.sh` — real 6-container Redis 7 Sentinel cluster: 1 primary + 2 replicas + 3 sentinels): sentinel reports the master; **connection-pool starvation/recovery** (single-connection pool times out concurrent ops, recovers after release); **TTL expiry** (key deleted by real Redis); **stream XTRIM MINID trim + purge-on-drain**; **Sentinel round-trip** (client resolves elected master, writes/reads); **FAILOVER demo: killing the primary → Sentinel promotes a replica → the go-redis failover client transparently follows the new master**; AOF (`appendonly=yes`) + RDB save points confirmed live
  - **SECURITY CHECKPOINT PASSED:** no plaintext PII can enter Redis as a key (validated builders reject E.164/OTP/email shapes — enforced by tests at the construction point, not just documentation); values remain bcrypt-hashed OTP codes / SHA-256 token digests / ciphertext envelopes only; the chart carries no credentials (secret-injected)
  - **Post-review hardening (code-reviewer findings, all fixed + re-verified):** the Helm chart liveness/readiness probes now run via `sh -c` with a password-or-nothing branch — K8s does **not** expand `$(VAR)` in probe `exec.command` args, and `redis-cli -a ""` errors when no password is set (probes also handle the no-secret dev case); the startup Redis probe is now **fail-fast in production** (identity/relay return an error when Redis is unreachable at boot, like the PG migration) while staying non-fatal in dev; the live pool-starvation test is now deterministic — it acquires and holds a dedicated `redis.Conn` (no BLPOP-scheduling race) instead of racing the single pool connection; duplicate `blindHashRe`/`sha256HexRe` regexes consolidated into one `hex64Re`; relay `Enqueue`/`Len` PII-rejection guard test added; live-script cleanup loop guards the empty-array case
  - **Decisions & notes:** go-redis v9 has no `HealthCheckInterval` option (v10 API) — idle-connection health is go-redis's built-in checkout check, and our `Ping` probe covers startup readiness; `ErrPoolTimeout` is unexported in v9 (internal/pool), so the live pool test asserts the `connection pool timeout` message; real Redis keeps an empty stream after XDEL, so purge-on-drain is the relay queue's explicit DEL (asserted in tests); Sentinel needs a **writable** config file (it persists its own state) — reflected in both the chart (config copied to a writable volume) and the live script; `karma`/`vote_buffer`/`analyst_load`/`rate` namespaces are registered with TTLs now and adopted by their services in later phases
